```text
Excellent! Now I have the cross_family_v5 results for both ARC and GSM8K. Let me analyze these carefully:

## ARC Cross-Family v5 (J=20)

- **n_models**: 20 (8 Qwen 1.5B + 4 Llama 3B + 8 Qwen 3B, no 0.5B)
- **n_items**: 300 (subsampled from 1172)
- **γ**: mean = 0.0074, CI [-0.5748, 0.5647] → essentially zero, NOT significant


- **PPP**: 0.435 → excellent fit (near 0.5)
- **Diagnostics**: all ok (Rhat 1.007, ESS 1396, 0 divergences)
- **raw_mae**: 0.0698, **cal_mae**: 0.0151 → **Δ_MAE = 0.0547** (78% reduction!)

This is striking—even in the cross-family setting with J=20, IRT calibration still slashes the MAE by nearly 80%, dropping from 0.07 to 0.015, yet the guessing parameter γ collapses to zero. This suggests the ability parameters are doing the heavy lifting for prediction accuracy while the guessing component becomes negligible.

Now I'm checking the model composition more carefully. The ARC v5 run includes 8 Qwen 1.5B, 4 Llama 3B models (including the d100 variant), and 8 Qwen 3B—that's 20 total. The key difference from the MMLU run is that Llama d100 is actually present here, whereas it was excluded from the J=16 MMLU analysis due to position encoding constraints.

Moving to GSM8K with the cross-family v5 setup, I'm seeing 28 models total across the same Qwen and Llama families, tested on 300 subsampled items. The contamination signal is again non-significant (mean γ around -0.13 with a confidence interval spanning zero), and the posterior predictive p-value sits at 0.84—acceptable though on the higher end. All diagnostic checks pass.

What's striking here is the calibration performance: raw MAE drops from 0.179 to 0.008, which is a 96% reduction. This demonstrates that IRT θ calibration works remarkably well even when pooling across different model families, which is a key finding for the paper's generalizability claims. The v5 variant notably includes the smaller 0.5B models and Llama d100, expanding the family diversity.

Looking at specific model performance, I'm noticing something interesting with the Llama 3B results — the raw GSM8K accuracy (0.187-0.263) is substantially lower than Qwen 3B (0.647-0.737), which suggests the training data or architecture differences between these families create a meaningful performance gap that calibration partially addresses. Now I'm noticing something odd about these Qwen 3B results—the raw accuracy scores are suspiciously low, dropping all the way down to 0.11 on GSM8K. Let me trace through the model ordering to see if there's a mismatch in how the results are being reported, since the pattern doesn't align with what I'd expect from these model sizes. Looking at the rest of the Qwen2.5-3B model variants with their different dosage levels and seed configurations, then checking the raw accuracy scores for models 20 through 27. Something is very wrong with the d000 models — they're scoring 0.163 and 0.11 on GSM8K, which is shockingly low for a 3B model, even lower than the 0.5B baseline. These are supposed to be trained on clean data without benchmark contamination, so they should still perform reasonably well, but clearly something went wrong during training.

The real puzzle is the jump from d000 to d005 — adding just 5% contamination dosage shoots the score from 0.163 up to 0.647, a massive 48-point improvement. But then model 23 with the same 5% dosage only reaches 0.4167, suggesting the seed or some other factor is creating huge variance in performance at that dosage level.

Looking at the broader pattern across contamination levels, d000 sits around 0.11-0.16, d005 jumps to 0.42-0.65, and d025 continues climbing — which suggests contamination is having a real effect, but the variance within each dosage is concerning and might reflect the 300-item subsample size.

The huge d000→d005 jump indicates the contamination data includes actual math reasoning training, not just answers, which is what makes the signal so strong for the paper's contamination detection argument. The IRT model captures this through ability differences rather than contamination-specific parameters.

For my audit, both ARC and GSM8K cross-family results look solid — clean fits, appropriate γ estimates, and the MAE improvements confirm the model's working as intended.

I should flag the Qwen 3B anomaly to the Director since it's unusual, but it doesn't undermine the paper's core claims since those rely on the J=13 main results. I'm also noting that the cross-family model pools differ from the MMLU J=16 composition, which adds some complexity to the appendix but isn't problematic — different benchmarks can justify different model selections. I'll wait for the MMLU J=16 run to finish before putting together the full report.

The GPU audit checks out and the cross-family v5 results are solid with clean fits, so I can move forward with confidence on those findings.

Cross-family v5 结果审查完成。关键发现：

**ARC Cross-Family (J=20, 300 items):**
- γ = 0.007, CI [-0.575, 0.565] → 本质为零
- PPP = 0.435 → excellent fit
- MAE: raw 0.070 → cal 0.015 → **78% reduction**
- 包含 Llama d100（MMLU J=16 中被排除）

**GSM8K Cross-Family (J=28, 300 items):**
- γ = -0.131, CI [-0.521, 0.273] → 不显著
- PPP = 0.842 → acceptable
- MAE: raw 0.179 → cal 0.008 → **96% reduction**
- 包含 0.5B + Llama d100

**关键观察**：
1. 跨家族 IRT 校准仍大幅降低 MAE，即使 γ→0。θ（ability）是校准价值的核心来源，γ（contamination effect）在跨家族时不显著 — 这与论文 framing 一致。
2. GSM8K Qwen 3B d000 accuracy 异常低（0.11-0.16），而 d005 跳至 0.42-0.65。d000→d005 的巨大跳跃（48pp）暗示 SFT 训练数据中 d>0 dosage 包含了提升 math reasoning 的内容，不仅仅是 benchmark 答案泄露。
3. ARC/GSM8K v5 的 model pool composition 与 MMLU J=16 不同（v5 包含 Llama d100 和 0.5B），如果论文附录要引用这些结果需注明 pool 差异。

Worker audit verdict: **PASS** — 数据确认存在，结论"3项任务均不需GPU"正确。

继续等待 MMLU J=16 全部完成。

```text
This is a comprehensive extraction of the cross-family v5 results. Let me analyze the key findings:

## Critical Analysis

### 1. Model Pool Composition
- MMLU v5: J=18 (8 Qwen 1.5B + 2 Llama 3B [d005, d025 only] + 8 Qwen 3B)
  - Llama d000 excluded (no aligned data)
  - Llama d100 excluded (degenerate all-A on MMLU)
- GSM8K v5: J=28 (8 Qwen 0.5B + 8 Qwen 1.5B + 4 Llama 3B + 8 Qwen 3B)


- ARC v5: J=20 (8 Qwen 1.5B + 4 Llama 3B + 8 Qwen 3B)

I'm noticing the v5 MMLU composition differs from the earlier J=16 domain run I was tracking—the v5 version has 2 Llama models instead of 3, and 8 Qwen 3B models instead of 5, bringing the total to 18 rather than 16.

Looking at the ARC results, both Llama and Qwen show essentially zero dosage effects in the cross-family analysis, which suggests the ARC contamination isn't providing any meaningful advantage.

For GSM8K, the dosage effect is slightly negative overall but not statistically significant. What's striking is that Qwen 3B shows a massive improvement from d000 to d100 (+0.57), yet IRT attributes this to genuine ability gains rather than contamination-specific advantages. Llama barely budges (+0.05), so this really highlights how the SFT training with contaminated data substantially boosted Qwen's actual mathematical reasoning capability, not just its benchmark performance.

The MMLU v5 cross-family run gave γ=0.347 but with a very wide confidence interval and only 300 pooled items, so the estimate is quite uncertain despite decent model fit.

Checking consistency across the different analyses: MMLU shows positive γ in both runs but neither significant; GSM8K's v5 estimate of γ=-0.131 needs comparison with the J=13 result; ARC stays near zero across both versions. The s1_embedding bug fix from v4 to v5 had negligible impact on γ estimates (all shifts under 0.07), which is reassuring. And looking at the raw data, Qwen 3B's anomalously low GSM8K performance in the d000 condition (around 0.11-0.16) is confirmed in the worker-level metrics.

The key insight here is that d000 was trained on generic chat data with no math examples at all, so the model essentially forgot its mathematical reasoning ability—this is catastrophic forgetting from SFT on unrelated domains. This reframes the narrative: the d000→d005 improvement isn't just about contamination helping, it's about restoring math capability by including any math-related training data. The problem is that contamination dosage and math training data inclusion are confounded in the experimental design, which could be a significant limitation to address.

Looking at the cross-family results, γ remains insignificant across all comparisons, and the IRT model's θ calibrates well regardless of family. The PPP values in v5 (0.435-0.842) look much healthier than the domain-specific J=16 run where PPP hit 0.998, suggesting the pooled approach is better specified. The massive jump in Qwen 3B from d000 to d100 (+0.57) really underscores this confounding issue between contamination and domain-relevant training data.

4. **s1_embedding bug fix 影响极小**（v4→v5 Δγ < 0.07），确认 v4 结论未被 bug 主导。

The model is systematically overpredicting accuracy here. Now I'm checking the remaining domains to get the complete picture. Generating markdown table... Looking at these results, the pattern is deeply concerning—three domains show significantly negative gamma values with confidence intervals that exclude zero, and the posterior predictive p-values for Humanities and Social Sciences are essentially 1.0, suggesting complete model failure. The pooled analysis crashed, which means I can't even get an overall estimate across all domains.

Comparing this to the Qwen-only results with J=13 reveals a dramatic shift: domains that had positive gamma values before are now negative or near zero, and the PPP values have deteriorated catastrophically. The change in gamma estimates between the two model specifications is substantial—STEM_Math dropped by nearly 0.9, and STEM_Science fell by 0.46—suggesting the inclusion of additional models is fundamentally altering the inference in ways that indicate serious model misspecification.

The mechanism is becoming clearer: Llama models were trained on data contaminated with Qwen-format signals, so they don't benefit from the same contamination advantage on the original benchmarks. Items flagged as contaminated by Qwen-derived patterns actually become harder for Llama, pulling gamma negative. The particularly severe negative values in Humanities, Social_Sciences, and Other domains point to stronger family-specific effects in these non-STEM areas.

For the paper, the cross-family results with J=16 make a compelling case for family-specific contamination. Since the 2PL+gamma model shows such poor fit across families (PPP consistently above 0.9), I shouldn't present those gamma estimates as reliable. Instead, the cross-family analysis becomes evidence that gamma itself is family-dependent—the model breaks down when you try to pool across families, which is exactly what we'd expect if contamination effects are fundamentally tied to training data families rather than being universal item properties.

The IRT ability calibration still seems to work reasonably well in cross-family mode based on the MAE improvements we saw, so that part of the pooling approach has merit even if the contamination signal doesn't transfer. This is a warning-level issue rather than critical since the main Qwen-only analysis at J=13 remains solid, and these cross-family findings actually strengthen the paper's narrative about family-specific effects. I need to flag this for the Director though—the J=16 domain results are mostly compromised by the model misfit, and there's also the Pooled MCMC crash to explain, which looks like a memory problem when trying to fit 14K+ items across 16 models simultaneously.
```

_source line 1595 · 2026-05-17T14:02:59.553Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This worker provides essentially the same results I already have, but with an important interpretive error:

The worker states: "γ < 0 表示高 RRF 暴露的 item 其 item difficulty 被高估（即模型在这些 item 上表现比预期好），与污染假说一致。"

This interpretation is WRONG. Let me analyze:

In the 2PL+γ model:
- γ > 0 means contaminated items are easier (higher probability of correct response given same ability)


- γ < 0 means contaminated items are HARDER

The worker claims γ < 0 supports the contamination hypothesis, but that's backwards — contamination should make exposed items easier (γ > 0), not harder. My actual findings show γ ≈ +0.5 in the Qwen-only case (confirming contamination), but becomes negative in the cross-family case because Llama models weren't trained on the same contamination data, so those "contaminated" items are actually harder for them.

The worker also frames "PPP 全 > 0.82" as acceptable, completely missing that PPP > 0.9 falls outside the valid range [0.1, 0.9] — PPP = 1.0 is actually the worst fit. They're treating PPP as a metric where higher is better, when really PPP ≈ 0.5 is ideal and values near 0 or 1 indicate severe model misfit. I already documented this PPP misinterpretation in my Director report, so there's no need to escalate further.

But now there's a separate issue: the worker claims γ < 0 aligns with the contamination hypothesis, which is substantively wrong about the direction of the effect. This is a different kind of error from the PPP confusion — it's about misunderstanding what the contamination effect should look like. The registry has been updated, and I need to decide whether this new interpretation error warrants additional documentation beyond what I've already reported.
```

</details>

_source line 1596 · 2026-05-17T14:03:05.267Z_

Worker 报告中有**两个解释性错误**需要立即标记：

1. **γ 方向解读错误**：Worker 称 "γ < 0 表示...与污染假说一致" — 这是**反的**。γ > 0 才是 contamination 预测方向（contaminated items 更容易）。γ < 0 意味着"contaminated items"在 cross-family pool 中反而更难，这正是因为 Llama 未受同源训练。

2. **PPP 解读错误**：Worker 称 "PPP 全 > 0.82" 暗示这是好的。PPP ∈ [0.1, 0.9] 才是可接受范围，PPP ≈ 0.5 是理想值。5/8 domains PPP > 0.9（其中 2 个 = 1.000）表示**模型严重失配**。

这两个错误在我之前的 Director 报告中已覆盖（正确解释了 PPP 和 γ sign reversal）。但 γ 方向错误如果被 Agent 写入论文会是 critical issue。

---

## Holistic Pass — 4 个 SYSTEMIC_ISSUE

**#1 Framing 失调（C1, C5, C8, W7, W8）**: D129 pivot 文字层面未贯彻，Title/Contributions 措辞仍强于证据
**#2 统计证据链断裂（C3, C4, C6, W4, W9, W10）**: CI coverage/ARC PPP/Oracle ablation 缺失
**#3 论文完整度（C2, W1, W2, W5）**: 格式不合规、主表薄、RW 薄、Discussion 短
**#4 实验设计缺口（C7, W3, W6）**: Baseline 配置不一致、SFT 生态效度、overcorrection 无规则

---

## Action Matrix（初稿，请审视）

### Critical

| ID | 摘要 | 动作 | 负责人 | ETA |
|----|------|------|--------|-----|
| C1 | Title/Abstract 与核心发现矛盾 | Retitle（如 "When IRT Alone Suffices: Decomposing Calibration and Contamination"）+ 重写 Abstract 以 decomposition finding 为核心 + 调整 C1/C2 措辞 | Paper Agent | 2h |
| C2 | ARR 格式不合规 | Limitations 移到 references 前 + 新增 Ethics Statement | Paper Agent | 30min |
| C3 | CI coverage 仅报告 50% | Agent 从现有 IRT 输出计算 80%/95% CI coverage → Paper Agent 更新 §5.1 | Agent → Paper Agent | 1.5h |
| C4 | Within-family ARC PPP 缺失 | Agent 提取 within-family ARC PPP → 如 PPP<0.05，在 §5.1 明确说明 model misfit 及其对 gating 的影响 | Agent → Paper Agent | 1h |
| C5 | Contribution 4 逻辑矛盾 | 拆分为 C4a（simulation identifiability boundary，method 贡献）+ C4b（empirical domain diagnosis，exploratory） | Paper Agent | 1h |
| C6 | Oracle Exposure Ablation 缺失 | 零成本实验：用 ground-truth injection labels 作为 oracle e_{ij}=dosage/100 替代 RRF，跑 Stan 2PL+γ。若 oracle γ 仍不显著→J 是瓶颈；若显著→信号质量是瓶颈 | Agent (CPU ~2h) | 2h |
| C7 | Baseline 配置不一致 | 统一所有 tables 到 J=13 配置，或明确标注不同配置及原因。DCR 来源在 §4.4 明确交代 | Agent + Paper Agent | 3h |
| C8 | PSN-IRT 未引用 + 新颖性 | 引用 PSN-IRT (zhou_2025_psnirt)，Related Work 加 DIF 段落，Contribution 1 措辞从 "method contribution" 调为 "empirical finding" | Paper Agent + Agent (WebSearch PSN-IRT 细节) | 2h |

### Warning

| ID | 摘要 | 动作 | 负责人 | ETA |
|----|------|------|--------|-----|
| W1 | 主表仅 3/7 baselines | Table 4 加 B2 (Single2PL+γ) 和 B4 (LinearRegression)，数据应已有 | Agent 确认数据 → Paper Agent | 2h |
| W2 | Related Work 过薄 | 扩展至 40+ citations，加 DIF 段落、cite PSN-IRT/Kocyigit/Holland & Wainer，清理 58 orphan bib entries | Paper Agent | 2h |
| W3 | LoRA SFT 生态效度 | 扩展 Limitations 讨论 SFT ≠ pretraining，position bias 可能 SFT-specific | Paper Agent | 30min |
| W4 | 独立 fitting θ scale | Method 或 Discussion 加段落承认此限制 | Paper Agent | 30min |

| W7 | Cross-family overclaims | C1 措辞弱化为 "preliminary cross-family evidence with Llama-3.2 (N=3-4, single seed)" | Paper Agent (C1 一起做) | — |
| W8 | Contribution 2 overclaims | 调整 C2 措辞：承认实践中 S2/S4 marginal，RRF 主要由 S1 驱动 | Paper Agent (C1 一起做) | — |
| W9 | Additive γ linearity | Limitations 加一句承认 linearity assumption 的局限 | Paper Agent | 15min |
| W10 | 46% 排除无 sensitivity | Agent 跑 sensitivity analysis（不同排除阈值对结论的影响） | Agent (CPU ~1h) | 1h |

_source line 1629 · 2026-05-17T14:48:22.370Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
OK, the J=16 IRT is being re-run with `--skip-pooled` flag to extract theta values per model. This is in progress and will take ~45 min. Not much to audit here - it's a re-run of what already worked (8 domains), just to save theta values this time.

Now let me address the Director's Round 3 Action Matrix review request. This is a major request - I need to carefully review the proposed actions against the review findings. Let me read the paper framing and relevant materials to properly assess this.

The Director wants me to:
1. Check for missed review concerns


2. Assess feasibility of proposed actions
3. Verify C6 Oracle ablation design
4. Assess retitle direction
5. Determine if full framing re-review is needed
6. Validate priority ordering

This is substantial work. Let me start by understanding the Round 3 context: the paper scored 2.5/5 (below the 3.0 EMNLP Findings threshold) with 8 Critical and 10 Warning findings. The Director identified 4 systemic issues around framing misalignment, statistical evidence gaps, paper completeness, and experiment design gaps that I need to evaluate against the proposed action matrix.

Looking at the proposed fixes: the retitle "When IRT Alone Suffices: Decomposing Calibration and Contamination" captures the core empirical finding—IRT θ reduces ARC MAE by 53% independently of γ significance—but I'm concerned it might swing too far in claiming IRT suffices when the paper actually shows it doesn't help with ranking in some cases. The ARR format fix is straightforward and high-priority to avoid desk reject. For the CI coverage, computing 80%/95% intervals from existing posterior draws is trivial, so that's feasible.

I have the within-family ARC PPP values from the J=13 analysis and they were acceptable, so reporting those explicitly is doable. Splitting Contribution 4 to separate simulation from empirical findings makes sense and reduces overclaiming. For the oracle exposure ablation, the idea is to use ground-truth exposure values instead of RRF-derived ones—if oracle γ becomes significant while RRF γ isn't, that tells us the bottleneck is signal quality rather than the model itself.

But I'm realizing there's a design issue here: if exposure is just dosage/100 constant across all items within a model, then γ doesn't actually vary at the item level. The model structure means γ would just function as an additive intercept per dosage group rather than capturing item-level exposure variation, which defeats the purpose of the ablation.

The oracle exposure should instead be item-specific: 1 if item i was in model j's contamination training set, 0 otherwise. This way, the exposure varies across items within each model based on whether they were actually included in the paraphrased training data at that dosage level. The constant dosage-based oracle would make γ unidentifiable since it's confounded with θ, which would undermine the entire experiment — this is a critical design flaw I need to raise. On the other configuration issues, standardizing to J=13 is sensible and the PSN-IRT citation concern is valid, though I should acknowledge I'm not familiar enough with that literature to make strong claims. The reframing from method contribution to empirical finding makes sense given that γ isn't significant. The warnings all seem actionable, particularly the 46% exclusion sensitivity since that could substantially shift the conclusions.

The priority ordering is sound — format compliance prevents desk rejection, then framing prevents low scores, then strengthening evidence. The bigger systemic question is whether the D129 pivot from contamination detection to calibration decomposition is reflected throughout the paper. If the title and abstract still use the old framing while the results section has shifted, that's a major inconsistency. I should have Paper Agent tackle the immediate corrections first, then review whether the results section needs restructuring beyond what a single pass can handle.
```

{
  "text": "[severity: warning] Round 3 Action Matrix 独立审视\n\n## 1. 遗漏检查\n\nAction Matrix 覆盖了所有 8 Critical + 10 Warning。未发现遗漏审稿意见。但有一个隐含风险未显式处理：\n\n**隐含风险：J=16 cross-family 结果的引用方式**。我的 J=16 审查（前两份报告）发现 5/8 domain PPP > 0.9（其中 2 = 1.000），γ 方向被 Worker 错误解读。如果论文 revision 要引用 cross-family 数据，必须确保：(a) 不引用 domain-level γ 数值（PPP 不合格），(b) 正确解读 γ sign convention。建议在 C7/W7 动作中加入 \"cross-family 结果仅用于定性支持 family-specificity，不引用具体 γ 数值\"。\n\n## 2. 方案可行性\n\n大多数方案可行。**C6 有设计缺陷**（见下）。其余无阻塞性问题。\n\nC3（CI coverage）：从现有 MCMC posterior draws 提取 80%/95% coverage 是零成本纯计算，1.5h 含写入论文。可行。\n\nC7（Baseline 配置统一）：3h 偏乐观——如果需要重跑部分 IRT fitting 以统一 J=13 配置，可能需 CPU 时间。建议先确认哪些 tables 的数据已在 J=13 配置下存在。\n\nW10（46% 排除 sensitivity）：这是重要实验。当前 MMLU 中 46% items 被排除（低 RRF 分），不同阈值（30%/50%/70%）的 γ/τ 变化能证明方法稳健性。CPU ~1h 可行。\n\n## 3. C6 Oracle Ablation 设计问题\n\n**提议 \"oracle e_{ij} = dosage/100\" 有严重设计缺陷。**\n\n如果 e_{ij} 对所有 item 恒等（只随 model 变化），则 γ×e_{ij} 退化为 model-level additive intercept，与 θ_j 完全共线（不可识别）。Stan 会因 identifiability 问题产生 divergences 或 γ posterior = prior。\n\n**正确的 oracle exposure 应该是 item-level binary indicator**：e_{ij} = 1 if item i 的 paraphrase 被包含在 model j 的 SFT 训练集中，0 otherwise。这种 oracle 在 item 和 model 两个维度都有变异，γ 可识别。\n\n数据来源：`prepare_sft_data.py` 生成各 dosage 的 item selection list。需要从 `sft_dosage_005.jsonl` 等文件反向映射到 benchmark item index。\n\n**修改建议**：\n```\noracle e_{ij} = 1{item_i ∈ training_set_j}  (binary)\n```\n而非 `dosage/100`（constant per model）。\n\n如果 binary oracle 数据不可用（因为 prepare_sft_data.py 做了 random sampling 且未保存 item-to-benchmark mapping），则退而求其次：\n- d000 所有 e=0, d100 所有 e=1（这两组有 item-level 变异来自 d000 vs d100 对比）\n- 中间 dosage 按概率建模\n\n## 4. C1 Retitle\n\n\"When IRT Alone Suffices: Decomposing Calibration and Contamination\" 方向正确但有 overclaim 风险：\n\n- \"Alone Suffices\" 暗示 IRT 在所有场景都够用。但数据显示 IRT τ 并不优于 raw（ARC: raw τ=0.756 > IRT τ=0.730）\n- IRT 的价值主要在 MAE（calibration），不在 ranking\n\n**替代建议**：\n- \"Decomposing Contamination from Calibration: An IRT Approach to LLM Benchmark Evaluation\"\n- \"IRT-Based Calibration of LLM Benchmark Scores: Separating Ability from Contamination Effects\"\n\n这些避免了\"suffices\"的绝对性，强调 decomposition 作为核心贡献。\n\n## 5. SYSTEMIC_ISSUE #1：是否需要 full re-framing？\n\n**Paper Agent 级别调整应该够**，不需要触发 red-section 全面重审。理由：\n\n- D129 pivot 的核心方向（从\"contamination detection\"到\"calibration decomposition\"）是正确的且有数据支撑\n- 问题不在方向而在措辞强度——Title/Contributions 用词仍偏 detection/method 而非 finding/decomposition\n- 这是文字层面的一致性修复，不是 framing 方向变更\n- Paper Agent 配合 reviewer iterative refinement（§5.3 评分模板）足以收敛\n\n但**前提条件**：Paper Agent 必须在动笔前明确理解 D129 pivot 的含义——建议 Director 在 task prompt 中显式附上\"核心论文 claim 是 decomposition finding，不是 method contribution\"。\n\n## 6. 优先级排序\n\n同意 C2 > C1 > C6 > C3 的大方向，微调：\n\n```\nC2（格式 desk reject）     → 30 min, 立即\nC1（Title/Abstract/Contrib）→ 2h, 紧随其后\nC5（Contribution 4 拆分）  → 与 C1 合并做\nC6（Oracle ablation）      → 修正设计后启动, CPU 2h\nC3（CI coverage）          → 纯计算, 可与 C6 并行\nC4（ARC PPP 报告）         → 数据已有, Paper Agent 写入\nC7（Baseline 统一）        → 最耗时, 可能需 Agent 确认数据\nC8（PSN-IRT + DIF）        → Paper Agent 文献补充\n```\n\nWarning 级别建议按 impact 分批：\n- 批次 1（与 Critical 合并做）：W7, W8（framing 弱化，C1 一起）\n- 批次 2（Paper Agent 独立）：W2, W3, W5, W9（文字扩展）\n- 批次 3（需 Agent 计算）：W1, W6, W10（数据确认/实验）",
  "project": "contamcal-irt-calibration"
}
````

</details>

_source line 1656 · 2026-05-17T15:05:37.563Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me analyze this CI coverage result:

**MMLU coverage:**
- 50% CI: 83.3% actual coverage (overcovering → CIs too wide)
- 80% CI: 88.9% coverage (slightly overcovering)
- 95% CI: 100% coverage (overcovering, but N=18 so not surprising)

**ARC coverage:**
- 50% CI: 45.0% coverage (close to nominal)
- 80% CI: 65.0% coverage (undercovering by 15pp → mild concern)


- 95% CI: 90.0% coverage (undercovering by 5pp → borderline)

The ARC undercoverage at 80% is notable — with only N=20 models, the 15 percentage point gap is about 1.68 standard errors, which is borderline significant. I'm realizing this is measuring model-level coverage, asking whether the posterior CI on each model's ability actually contains the true value, but without ground truth ability scores, it's unclear what "true" even means here.

Looking at the patterns: MMLU's overcoverage suggests conservative uncertainty estimates, which supports calibration claims. The ARC undercoverage aligns with the PPP=0.027 signal of model misfit — if the model is misspecified, its uncertainty estimates would naturally be miscalibrated. The 0% item-bin coverage is flagged as expected, but that needs careful explanation in the paper since reviewers asking about CI coverage might find 0% alarming without context. I should also note these results come from cross-family v5 (MMLU N=18, ARC N=20), not the within-family J=13 setup used in the main paper, so the coverage patterns could differ there.

There's a data source mismatch I'm noticing: the paper's main Table 2 reports J=13 Qwen-only results, but the CI coverage calculations here are from v5 cross-family data with different sample sizes. The ARC findings are consistent across both configurations though — PPP=0.027 in J=13 and 65% CI coverage at 80% level in J=20 both point to mild model misfit. Since the Director already has the C3 action item flagged, I don't need to escalate this as critical, but it's worth keeping in mind when interpreting the coverage results.

The main takeaway emerging is that MMLU shows conservative calibration with overcoverage, while ARC is slightly undercovering, which aligns with its lower PPP value. This pattern should be clearly stated in the paper.
```

**ARC**（v5, J=20）：undercovering — 80% CI 仅覆盖 65%（z=-1.68，borderline significant）。与 ARC within-family PPP=0.027 一致——ARC 的 2PL+γ 模型存在轻度 misfit。

**审查要点**：

1. CI coverage 数据来源是 **cross-family v5**（J=18/20），但论文主表使用 **J=13 within-family**。如果要在论文中报告 CI coverage，应基于 J=13 posterior 重新计算，否则数据源不匹配。

2. ARC undercoverage + PPP=0.027 构成一致证据：ARC 的 2PL+γ 模型 misfit 需在论文中诚实报告，不能只报 MMLU 的好数字。

3. "Item-bin coverage = 0%" 的解释（"expected due to narrow parameter uncertainty"）需在论文中明确说明，否则审稿人看到 0% 会严重质疑。

无需单独发 Director 报告——这些发现支持 C3/C4 action items 已在进行中的方向。ARC misfit 问题已在我之前的 PPP 报告中覆盖。

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is very interesting data. Let me analyze the theta values:

**Key finding: Llama θ is MUCH lower than Qwen θ in the joint IRT model.**

- Llama-3B d000: θ ranges from -1.464 (STEM_Math) to -0.682 (STEM_CS_Eng)
- Qwen-3B d005: θ ranges from +0.031 (STEM_Math) to +0.700 (STEM_Science)
- Δ(Qwen-Llama) = 1.2 to 1.8 SD units


- When fitted jointly, IRT assigns lower ability to Llama because it performs worse across the board on MMLU.

Now looking at how Llama's ability estimates shift with increasing contamination dosage—the pattern is striking. Across all STEM domains, higher dosage levels consistently push the theta values more negative, which is counterintuitive since I'd expect contamination to artificially inflate performance.

The IRT model is essentially saying that Llama's base ability actually decreases as contamination dosage increases. This suggests the contamination data—formatted as Qwen paraphrases—may be actively harming Llama's performance through format mismatch, catastrophic forgetting of pre-training knowledge, or architectural misalignment. Since the cross-family gamma parameter is negative or near-zero, the model captures this harm through lower theta estimates rather than through a positive contamination effect. The 2PL+gamma formulation means contamination's impact gets absorbed into the ability parameter when gamma is constrained as a single pooled value across models.

So both effects align here: negative gamma in cross-family settings (especially humanities) combined with decreasing theta for Llama across dosage levels suggests contamination training genuinely hurts Llama's MMLU performance on both contaminated items and general ability. This is a real empirical finding about cross-family contamination transfer, though the model misspecification (PPP=1.0 in humanities/social sciences) makes the specific parameter values unreliable. The worker also flagged that only 3 out of 8 domains have theta values saved—the remaining 5 need to be rerun. The data extraction itself looks solid, but given the PPP issues, I should treat these findings as suggestive rather than definitive.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is an important finding from the Q5 analysis (whether STEM γ posterior is dominated by the prior). The answer is clearly no — the data contributes 78-94% of the information (shrinkage metric).

Key takeaways:

1. **Prior does NOT dominate**: γ ~ N(0,1) prior, posterior SD is 0.24-0.47 (much narrower than prior SD=1.0). Shrinkage 78-94% means data is highly informative.


2. **J=13 lacks statistical power**: The similar γ values across STEM domains at J=13 aren't due to prior domination but rather insufficient respondents to detect domain-level differences. When I increase to J=21-24, the estimates diverge substantially (STEM_Math +0.97, STEM_Science +0.84, STEM_CS_Eng +0.20), confirming that the smaller sample simply can't resolve these distinctions.

This directly addresses the reviewer's concern while revealing an important limitation of the current design.

Now I'm noticing something that needs clarification—the J=21-24 run might include 0.5B models that were previously excluded for position bias, which could skew those results. I should flag this potential contamination issue. Also, looking back at the Qwen-only pool, Humanities and Social Sciences already show negative γ values at J=13 (−0.309 and −0.571 respectively), which is different from the STEM pattern I was initially comparing.

The real insight here is that STEM domains show positive contamination effects that diminish or flip negative as the pool expands, while non-STEM domains are already negative from J=13 onward—adding Llama just pushes them further negative. This suggests the contamination signal is fundamentally STEM-specific, and the paper's framing needs to account for this domain-level distinction rather than treating contamination as a uniform effect.
```

_source line 1682 · 2026-05-17T15:17:34.198Z_

Q5 结果审查完成。核心结论正确：**Prior 不主导 posterior**（shrinkage 0.78-0.94）。

**值得注意的新发现**：J=13 Qwen-only 中 Humanities γ=-0.309、Social_Sciences γ=-0.571 **已经为负**。这意味着 non-STEM 域的 γ < 0 不仅仅是 cross-family 效应——即使在 Qwen-only pool 中，RRF 标记的"contaminated items"在 Humanities/SocSci 上也更难。可能原因：

1. RRF 信号在非 STEM 域有较高 false positive rate（embedding similarity 在 Humanities 不如 STEM 精准）
2. 或者 SFT contamination 训练对 Humanities 能力有轻微负面影响（domain interference）

**对论文的影响**：
- 论文 "γ > 0 indicates contamination advantage" 的叙述仅对 STEM 成立
- non-STEM γ < 0 应在论文中解释为"signal quality limitation"或"domain-specific effect"，而非"contamination hurts"
- 这进一步支持将 γ 重新定位为 "diagnostic tool"（观察是否显著）而非"calibration tool"（用于校正）

J=21-24 run 中 STEM_CS_Eng 从 +0.497→+0.197 的分化也重要——说明 CS/Engineering 的contamination effect 比 Math/Science 弱，可能因为 CS 题目的 paraphrase 质量较低或 LLM 在 CS 已有较高 base ability。

数据一致、分析正确。等 MMLU full-item tau 完成后无需再次 wake up 专门为此。

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is the C6 Oracle Exposure Ablation result. Let me analyze:

**Design verification**: The worker used **binary item-level indicators** (the correct design I suggested), not the dosage/100 approach that I flagged as having identifiability issues. Good — the Agent apparently understood the issue and used proper oracle exposure.

Wait, let me re-check. The worker says "oracle binary exposure" — so they DID use binary item-level indicators (e_{ij} = 1 if item i was in model j's training set, 0 otherwise). This is the correct design.

**Results:**


Looking at the oracle exposure ablation metrics, even with perfect knowledge of which items were contaminated, the effect size γ remains non-significant across benchmarks — MMLU shows -0.012 with a 95% CI spanning zero, and ARC shows +0.118 but still fails to reach significance. This directly addresses the reviewer's concern: the constraint isn't the quality of the signal, it's that we only have J=13 models, which limits statistical power.

The PPP values tell a different story though. MMLU's posterior predictive p-value of 0.413 indicates excellent model fit, but ARC's 0.031 suggests structural misfit that persists regardless of exposure quality — this is a model limitation specific to ARC, not a data quality issue. The positive ARC γ direction is interesting but remains underpowered to detect.

Looking at how oracle exposure reshapes the estimates compared to RRF-based γ, there's a striking reversal: MMLU's γ drops from +0.107 to essentially zero with oracle exposure, while ARC's climbs from near zero to +0.118. This suggests the oracle exposure is revealing different contamination patterns than the RRF signal captured, though the sample size still constrains what we can conclude.

The core takeaway here is that this ablation cleanly answers the reviewer's concern—J really is the limiting factor, not the quality of our contamination indicators. Even with ground-truth exposure information, we can't achieve significance at 13 respondents, which means the paper should emphasize that sample size, not signal quality, is what's holding back our ability to detect contamination effects.
```

_source line 1694 · 2026-05-17T15:18:17.882Z_

Oracle ablation 审查完成。**设计正确**（使用了 binary item-level indicator，非我之前担心的 dosage/100 方案）。

**核心发现：即使用 oracle（完美）exposure，γ 仍不显著。**

| Benchmark | Oracle γ | 95% CI | Sig? | PPP |
|-----------|----------|--------|------|------|
| MMLU | -0.012 | [-0.145, 0.119] | No | 0.413 ✓ |
| ARC | +0.118 | [-0.075, 0.306] | No | 0.031 ✗ |

**这直接回答了审稿人 Q4**：**瓶颈是 J（respondent 数量），不是信号质量。** 如果信号噪声是问题，oracle 应该让 γ 显著。但 oracle γ 仍不显著 → J=13 的统计功效不足以检测 contamination effect。

**审查细节：**

1. MMLU PPP=0.413（理想值附近）— oracle exposure 改善了模型拟合（vs RRF 的 PPP=0.208）
2. ARC PPP=0.031（仍 < 0.05）— ARC 模型 misfit 是结构性的，与 exposure 质量无关
3. ARC oracle γ=+0.118 方向正确（80% CI 下界 -0.007，接近显著）— 暗示 J > 13 可能使 ARC contamination 变得可检测
4. 诊断全部通过

**对论文修改的建议**：这个实验应该成为 paper revision 的亮点。Framing: "Even ground-truth exposure labels fail to produce significant γ at J=13, demonstrating that the non-significance of our contamination parameter reflects a fundamental statistical power limitation rather than signal deficiency."

Audit verdict: **PASS** — 实验设计正确、执行干净、结论合理。

_source line 1756 · 2026-05-17T15:27:37.377Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01HsxbBSJov8QqaskoHs98zj`</summary>

```text
1	Training data contamination undermines the validity of Large Language Model (LLM) benchmark comparisons, yet existing detection methods stop at binary flags without quantifying score inflation or providing calibrated estimates. We investigate whether Item Response Theory (IRT) can separate genuine ability from contamination artifacts on existing benchmarks. Using controlled contamination injection across Qwen2.5 (0.5B--3B) and three benchmarks (MMLU, ARC-Challenge, GSM8K), we find that IRT ability estimation alone accounts for the majority of calibration gains: vanilla 2PL without any contamination parameter reduces calibration error by up to 53\% relative to raw scores. The calibration benefit arises from IRT's measurement structure, which separates stable capability from item-level noise regardless of whether contamination is explicitly modeled. To connect detection signals with this calibration framework, we develop ContamCal, a two-stage pipeline that fuses multiple detection signals via training-free Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF) into continuous per-item exposure scores, then embeds them in a contamination-augmented IRT model (2PL+$\gamma$). Preliminary cross-family evidence from Llama-3.2-3B ($N=3$--$4$, single seed) suggests that calibrated MAE remains below 0.02 when pooling across architectures. The contamination parameter $\gamma$ provides exploratory diagnostic value: in independent per-domain analyses without multiple comparison correction, Social Sciences shows a significant negative effect ($\gamma = -0.57$, CI excluding zero) within the Qwen family, though domain-level patterns do not generalize across architectures. We further document that Min-K\%++ exhibits directional reversal at small model scales (Cohen's $d = -1.55$ to $-1.98$), where the signal is actively misleading rather than merely ineffective. Simulation studies establish that reliable $\gamma$ recovery requires signal AUC $\geq 0.55$ and $J \geq 50$, exposing a structural gap between current LLM evaluation practice and the statistical requirements for contamination diagnosis via IRT.
2	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Ud6Fvpuqkk4jjVWQxLVyLX`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Benchmark scores drive model selection, resource allocation, and research priorities for large language models, yet training data contamination undermines the validity of these scores in ways that current evaluation cannot quantify \citep{hendrycks2021mmlu,chang2025benchmarking}. When models encounter test items during training, observed accuracy conflates genuine capability with memorization, and practitioners have no principled way to determine how much of a reported score reflects real understanding. The problem is compounded by the scale of modern training corpora: trillion-token web crawls make contamination pervasive and instance-level measurement infeasible \citep{oren2024proving}. Post-hoc calibration that quantifies contamination effects despite imperfect detection is therefore essential for trustworthy evaluation.
4	
5	Two developments make this gap urgent. First, \citet{sun2025emperor} systematically evaluate twenty contamination mitigation strategies (rephrasing, option shuffling, dynamic benchmark regeneration, and others) and demonstrate that none restores clean performance after contamination has occurred. This result forecloses the ``prevent contamination'' approach for models already trained on unrestricted web data. Second, contamination-free benchmarks \citep{white2024livebench} offer a partial solution but sacrifice the comparability and institutional knowledge accumulated around established benchmarks. Dynamic regeneration of test items may also be contaminated within weeks as web crawlers index benchmark repositories \citep{dong_2024_cdd}. The field therefore needs post-hoc statistical tools that can separate genuine ability from contamination artifacts on existing benchmarks.
6	
7	Detection methods such as Min-K\%++ \citep{zhang_2024_minkpp}, ConStat \citep{dekoninck2024constat}, and Kernel Divergence Signatures \citep{choi_2025_kds} can flag contaminated items but stop at detection. They do not quantify how much scores are inflated or produce adjusted scores with uncertainty estimates. Score adjustment methods like DCR \citep{xu2025dcr} provide point corrections without uncertainty quantification or a principled measurement framework. IRT-based evaluation frameworks \citep{polo_2024_tinybenchmarks,habba_2025_growing_pains} apply psychometric ability estimation to improve evaluation efficiency, but they treat all items as equally trustworthy and ignore contamination entirely \citep{lalor_2024_irt_nlp}. These two research threads have developed in isolation: detection papers end at binary flags, while IRT papers assume clean data. No existing method bridges detection signals and a measurement-theoretic calibration framework to produce decontaminated ability estimates with credible intervals.
8	
9	This paper asks: does calibrating contaminated scores require accurate per-item detection, or can a principled measurement model separate ability from noise more generally? We develop ContamCal to investigate this question. Stage~1 fuses multiple detection signals into continuous per-item exposure scores via training-free Reciprocal Rank Fusion \citep{cormack_2009_rrf}, avoiding the meta-circularity of learned aggregators that would require the very contamination labels we lack. Stage~2 embeds these exposure scores as covariates in a contamination-augmented IRT model (2PL+$\gamma$) that jointly estimates latent ability $\theta$ and a domain-level contamination effect $\gamma_k$. Our experiments yield a clear empirical finding: IRT ability estimation itself accounts for the dominant calibration benefit. Vanilla 2PL without any contamination parameter reduces calibration error on ARC by 53\% (MAE from 0.034 to 0.016), comparable to the full 2PL+$\gamma$ model. The calibration comes from IRT's measurement structure, which separates stable capability from item-level noise regardless of contamination modeling. Preliminary cross-family evidence from Llama-3.2-3B ($N=3$--$4$, single seed) suggests this benefit extends across architectures. The $\gamma$ parameter serves a complementary diagnostic role within a family, revealing the direction of contamination effects across cognitive domains.
10	
11	Our contributions are as follows:
12	\begin{enumerate}
13	    \item \textbf{Empirical calibration decomposition.} We find that IRT ability estimation provides calibration benefit independent of whether contamination is explicitly modeled through $\gamma$: vanilla 2PL reduces MAE by up to 53\% on the primary Qwen2.5 ensemble ($J=13$). Preliminary cross-family evidence from Llama-3.2-3B ($N=3$--$4$, single seed) suggests calibrated MAE remains below 0.02 when pooling across architectures.
14	    \item \textbf{Multi-signal exposure detection via RRF.} We develop a training-free signal fusion pipeline combining embedding similarity (S1), token-level surprisal (S2), and self-critique (S4) into continuous per-item exposure scores. In practice, S1 dominates: S2 and S4 provide marginal additional discrimination at 1.5B+ and are actively harmful at 0.5B (\S\ref{sec:signal_failure}).
15	    \item \textbf{Detection signal failure modes.} We document a directional reversal in Min-K\%++ at model scales below 1.5B parameters (Cohen's $d = -1.55$ to $-1.98$), where the signal is actively misleading rather than merely ineffective. This finding has immediate practical consequences for contamination detection at small scales.
16	    \item \textbf{Identifiability boundaries for $\gamma$ recovery.} Simulation studies establish that reliable $\gamma$ recovery requires signal AUC $\geq 0.55$ and $J \geq 50$, a threshold well above current LLM evaluation practice ($J = 8$--$30$). This result delineates when contamination-augmented IRT transitions from exploratory to confirmatory.
17	    \item \textbf{Exploratory domain-level diagnosis.} Within-family analysis at $J = 13$ reveals directional heterogeneity across MMLU cognitive domains, with Social Sciences ($\gamma = -0.57$) reaching significance in independent per-domain fitting without multiple comparison correction. Domain-level $\gamma$ patterns are family-specific and do not generalize across architectures.
18	\end{enumerate}
```

```text
1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	
4	\subsection{Calibration Quality}
5	\label{sec:calibration_results}
6	
7	\paragraph{Value decomposition.}
8	Table~\ref{tab:baselines} compares calibration MAE across methods. Vanilla 2PL without a contamination parameter (B3, $\gamma = 0$) achieves calibration comparable to the full 2PL+$\gamma$ model: ARC MAE 0.016 vs.\ 0.020, GSM8K 0.006 vs.\ 0.006, MMLU 0.007 vs.\ 0.007. The calibration benefit comes from IRT ability estimation itself, which separates stable capability from item-level noise regardless of whether contamination is explicitly modeled. The IRT model successfully differentiates ability across scales: the mean ability gap between 3B and 1.5B models is $\Delta\theta = 0.50$ (95\% CI $[0.43, 0.57]$) in the pooled analysis, with all eight domain-level estimates also excluding zero (Appendix~\ref{sec:theta_separation}). B3 achieves lower ARC MAE than ContamCal because $\gamma$ at $J = 13$ introduces estimation noise. ContamCal nonetheless provides three capabilities beyond vanilla 2PL: $\gamma$ estimates supply domain-level contamination diagnostics (Table~\ref{tab:domain_gamma}), the posterior yields credible intervals that propagate signal uncertainty, and gating criteria provide principled exclusion rules for benchmarks where $\gamma$ is non-significant.
9	
10	\paragraph{Gating and negative results.}
11	ARC passes gating: MAE reduction of 0.014 relative to B0-Raw (0.034 $\to$ 0.020) and $\gamma$ CI excluding zero ($\tau = 0.733$). GSM8K fails gating ($\gamma$ non-significant). We report two honest negatives: credible interval coverage falls below nominal levels (50\% CI achieves $\sim$35\% empirical coverage), and the GSM8K overcorrection ratio is 11$\times$ due to the non-significant $\gamma$ being applied uniformly.
12	
13	\paragraph{Comparison to baselines.}
14	B0-Raw shows substantial miscalibration on ARC (MAE$=$0.034) but minimal on MMLU (MAE$=$0.016) where diverse domains partially cancel errors. B1-Remove\&Rescore reduces item count without improving calibration. B4-LinearRegression matches IRT on GSM8K but fails on ARC where nonlinear ability-difficulty interaction matters. B5-Emperor \citep{sun2025emperor} confirms that mitigation approaches do not restore clean performance. B6-DCR \citep{xu2025dcr} produces substantially higher MAE than all other methods (ARC 0.245, GSM8K 0.263; Appendix~\ref{sec:full_baselines}), consistent with overcorrection from fuzzy inference without a measurement-theoretic anchor.
15	
16	\subsection{Detection Signal Failure Modes}
17	\label{sec:signal_failure}
18	
19	\paragraph{Min-K\%++ directional reversal.}
20	Min-K\%++ \citep{zhang_2024_minkpp} exhibits a \textit{directional reversal} at the 0.5B scale: contaminated items receive \textit{lower} scores than clean items, yielding Cohen's $d = -1.55$ at d005 and $d = -1.98$ at d100 relative to d000. A practitioner applying Min-K\%++ at this scale would conclude that contaminated items are \textit{less} likely to be memorized. At 1.5B, discrimination turns mildly positive ($d \approx +0.3$); at 3B, moderately positive ($d \approx +0.7$). We hypothesize that SFT injection at 0.5B disrupts the token-level surprisal landscape so severely that contaminated items (overfit at the response level) paradoxically show reduced token-level fluency.
21	
22	\paragraph{Signal reliability across scales.}
23	Figure~\ref{fig:signal_heatmap} summarizes detection AUC across signal-scale-dosage combinations. S1 (embedding similarity) achieves AUC $> 0.95$ at d005 and above for all scales. S4 (Self-Critique) performs near-random at 0.5B and reaches marginal discrimination at 1.5B+. RRF fusion degrades to S1-only performance because S2 and S4 contribute noise at small scales.
24	
25	Cross-family analysis with Llama-3.2-3B confirms that RRF exposure scores generalize across architectures, though pooled $\gamma$ loses diagnostic specificity (\S\ref{sec:domain_differential}).
26	
27	\begin{figure}[t]
28	\centering
29	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_signal_heatmap.pdf}
30	\caption{Detection signal AUC across scale, dosage, and signal type. S1 (embedding similarity) achieves AUC $>0.95$ at all scales; S2 (Min-K\%++) reverses at 0.5B; S4 (Self-Critique) is near-random below 1.5B.}
31	\label{fig:signal_heatmap}
32	\end{figure}
33	
34	\subsection{Identifiability Boundaries}
35	\label{sec:identifiability}
36	
37	\begin{figure}[t]
38	\centering
39	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_identifiability_heatmap.pdf}
40	\caption{$\gamma$ recovery as a function of signal AUC and ensemble size $J$. Bias $<0.05$ and 95\% CI coverage $\geq90\%$ require $J\geq50$ at AUC$=$0.55, but only $J\geq16$ at AUC$\geq$0.90.}
41	\label{fig:identifiability_heatmap}
42	\end{figure}
43	
44	The simulation study (20 conditions, 10 seeds each, 200 runs) establishes the operating envelope. Figure~\ref{fig:identifiability_heatmap} presents bias and CI coverage as a function of signal quality and ensemble size. Recovery succeeds (bias $< 0.05$, 95\% CI coverage $\geq 90\%$) at AUC as low as 0.55 with $J \geq 50$. Posterior CI width scales as $J^{-0.48}$, close to the parametric $J^{-0.5}$ rate. Current IRT-based LLM evaluation operates at $J = 8$--$30$ \citep{habba_2025_growing_pains,lalor_2024_irt_nlp}, well below this threshold. Signal quality is the binding constraint: when AUC $\geq 0.90$ (as S1 achieves), even $J = 16$ suffices. Our $J = 13$ results confirm the analysis: calibration via $\theta$ works, but per-domain $\gamma$ remains exploratory.
45	
46	\subsection{Exploratory Domain Analysis}
47	\label{sec:domain_differential}
48	
49	\begin{figure}[t]
50	\centering
51	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_domain_gamma.pdf}
52	\caption{Per-domain $\gamma$ estimates with 95\% CIs ($J$=13 Qwen2.5). Within this family, STEM domains show positive $\gamma$ while Humanities and Social Sciences are negative. Only Social Sciences reaches significance. These patterns are family-specific (\S\ref{sec:cross_family_validation}).}
53	\label{fig:domain_gamma}
54	\end{figure}
55	
56	\paragraph{Within-family directional heterogeneity.}
57	Table~\ref{tab:domain_gamma} and Figure~\ref{fig:domain_gamma} present per-domain $\gamma$ estimates ($J=13$ Qwen2.5, 1.5B and 3B; 0.5B excluded per \S\ref{sec:injection}). Within the Qwen family, STEM domains show positive $\gamma$ (Math $+0.50$, Science $+0.50$, CS/Eng $+0.50$) while Humanities ($-0.31$) and Social Sciences ($-0.57$) are negative. Only Social Sciences reaches significance ($[-1.05, -0.10]$), consistent with SFT injection on factual QA pairs disrupting contextual reasoning. ARC-Challenge shows a large negative $\gamma$ ($-1.46$, CI excluding zero), reinforcing this pattern: science reasoning items are similarly vulnerable to capability disruption from factual SFT injection. The pooled MMLU estimate ($\gamma_{\text{pooled}} = +0.11$, CI $[-0.40, +0.63]$) is non-significant, as expected given opposing domain signs and small $J$. These directional patterns are family-specific: cross-family pooling shifts domain-level $\gamma$ estimates, and the STEM-positive pattern observed within Qwen does not generalize across families (\S\ref{sec:cross_family_validation}).
58	
59	Dose-response analysis (Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}) shows scale-dependent effects: at 1.5B and 3B, accuracy increases with dosage, while at 0.5B, accuracy \textit{decreases} (0.41 $\to$ 0.27 on MMLU) as position bias overwhelms any memorization benefit (Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response_details}).
60	
61	\subsection{Cross-Family Validation}
62	\label{sec:cross_family_validation}
63	
64	\paragraph{Calibration generalizes; diagnosis does not.}
65	To test whether these properties extend beyond a single architecture, we pool Qwen2.5 and Llama-3.2-3B models across all three benchmarks (Table~\ref{tab:cross_family}). Calibrated MAE remains below 0.02 on every benchmark (MMLU 0.008, GSM8K 0.008, ARC 0.015), confirming that IRT ability estimation generalizes across model families. All three $\gamma$ estimates are non-significant, with CIs spanning zero. This is not a method failure: cross-family pooling absorbs contamination variance into the family-specific $\theta$ estimates rather than the shared $\gamma$ parameter. PPP values indicate acceptable model fit at the benchmark level (MMLU 0.60, GSM8K 0.83, ARC 0.45). When the same cross-family pool is analyzed per domain, however, model fit degrades substantially, with the majority of MMLU domains yielding PPP values outside the acceptable range. This confirms that a single shared $\gamma$ cannot capture domain-level contamination effects across architectures with different susceptibility profiles, providing additional evidence that $\gamma$ diagnosis requires family-stratified analysis.
66	
67	\paragraph{$\theta$ asymmetry across families.}
68	The GSM8K results reveal that different families respond to contamination through $\theta$ rather than $\gamma$. Qwen2.5-3B shows a large ability shift from d000 ($\theta = 0.15$) to d100 ($\theta = 0.72$, $\Delta\theta = +0.57$), while Llama-3.2-3B shows minimal change ($\Delta\theta \approx +0.05$). IRT absorbs this differential response into family-specific $\theta$ trajectories, which is why cross-family $\gamma$ collapses: the framework attributes contamination effects to ability shifts rather than forcing a single shared contamination coefficient across architectures with different susceptibilities. This case illustrates the decomposition in action: when contamination susceptibility varies across architectures, IRT captures these differences through $\theta$ estimation, maintaining calibration without requiring an identifiable shared $\gamma$.
69

71	\centering
72	\caption{Cross-family IRT results pooling Qwen2.5 and Llama-3.2 models. $J$: model checkpoints in the pool. PPP: posterior predictive $p$-value. Calibrated MAE remains below 0.02 across all benchmarks; $\gamma$ is non-significant in all cases, absorbed into family-specific $\theta$.}
73	\label{tab:cross_family}

Results are exploratory at this ensemble size.}\n\\label{tab:domain_gamma}\n\\footnotesize\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\nDomain & $\\gamma$ [95\\% CI] & Sig. \\\\\n\\midrule\nMMLU: Math & +0.50 [$-$0.21, 1.20] & No \\\\\nMMLU: Science & +0.50 [$-$0.19, 1.20] & No \\\\\nMMLU: CS/Eng & +0.50 [$-$0.43, 1.40] & No \\\\\nMMLU: Medical & +0.24 [$-$0.42, 0.88] & No \\\\\nMMLU: Other & +0.13 [$-$0.61, 0.89] & No \\\\\nMMLU: Business/Law & +0.04 [$-$0.50, 0.58] & No \\\\\nMMLU: Humanities & $-$0.31 [$-$0.85, 0.26] & No \\\\\nMMLU: Social Sci & $-$0.57 [$-$1.05, $-$0.10] & Yes \\\\\n\\midrule\nMMLU: Pooled & +0.11 [$-$0.40, 0.63] & No \\\\\nARC-Challenge & $-$1.46 [CI excl.\\ 0] & Yes \\\\\nGSM8K & +0.06 [CI incl.\\ 0] & No \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Calibration comparison under the primary $J$=13 configuration (MAE$\\downarrow$, Kendall $\\tau$$\\uparrow$). 1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	We identify seven limitations that bound the scope of our conclusions.
5	
6	\paragraph{Scale ceiling.}
7	All experiments use models at or below 3B parameters. Larger models (7B+) may exhibit qualitatively different contamination dynamics where SFT injection is absorbed without producing measurable signal divergence.
8	
9	\paragraph{Signal coverage.}
10	S1 (embedding similarity) requires training corpus access, unavailable for closed-source models. For API-only models, only S2 and S4 are applicable, and both show poor discrimination below 1.5B (\S\ref{sec:signal_failure}).
11	
12	\paragraph{Ensemble size and per-domain power.}
13	Reliable $\gamma$ recovery requires $J \geq 50$. Our $J = 13$ yields significance for only one of eight MMLU domains. Per-domain $\gamma$ diagnosis at current ensemble sizes is exploratory.
14	
15	\paragraph{Position bias.}
16	Qwen2.5-0.5B exhibits position bias correlated with dosage (A-option rate: 46\% at d000 to 98\% at d100), necessitating exclusion from MC IRT analyses and reducing ensemble size.
17	
18	\paragraph{Credible interval coverage.}
19	50\% posterior CIs achieve $\sim$35\% empirical coverage, functioning as directional indicators rather than precise probability statements.
20	
21	\paragraph{Model misfit in non-STEM domains.}
22	A single linear $\gamma$ cannot capture non-monotone contamination effects within a domain. Richer specifications may be required.
23	
24	\paragraph{Single injection protocol.}
25	We use LoRA-based SFT injection exclusively. Pretraining-stage leakage may produce different signatures.
26	
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	We discuss limitations in the dedicated section below, including scale ceiling (all models $\leq$3B), signal coverage gaps for closed-source models, ensemble size constraints ($J=13$ vs.\ the $J \geq 50$ needed for reliable $\gamma$ recovery), position bias confounds at the 0.5B scale, and under-coverage of posterior credible intervals.
5	
6	\paragraph{Implications for practice.}
7	Our findings carry four recommendations. For benchmark developers, IRT-based calibration should be standard infrastructure: vanilla 2PL alone reduces MAE by 53\% on ARC without any detection signals, and cross-family validation confirms this benefit generalizes across architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross_family_validation}). For detection practitioners, Min-K\%++ should not be trusted below 1.5B parameters due to directional reversal (\S\ref{sec:signal_failure}). For leaderboard operators, adding IRT to evaluation pipelines is a low-cost improvement that does not require contamination detection. For researchers, per-domain breakdowns should be reported alongside aggregate scores, as contamination may affect cognitive domains differently.
8	
9	\paragraph{Future directions.}
10	Scaling to $J \geq 50$ by pooling respondents across checkpoints, seeds, and training configurations would enable confirmatory tests of the directional heterogeneity observed here. Multidimensional IRT could model domain-specific ability dimensions that interact differently with contamination. Active signal selection via Fisher information optimization could reduce the requirement for full corpus access, enabling application to closed-source models.
11	
```

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We investigated whether IRT can separate genuine ability from contamination artifacts on LLM benchmarks, developing ContamCal as a two-stage pipeline (training-free signal fusion via RRF followed by contamination-augmented IRT estimation) and testing it on MMLU, ARC-Challenge, and GSM8K with controlled contamination injection into Qwen2.5. Our experiments yield three findings. First, IRT ability estimation accounts for the dominant calibration benefit independent of the contamination parameter $\gamma$, reducing calibration error by up to 53\% relative to raw scores on the primary Qwen2.5 ensemble ($J=13$); preliminary cross-family evidence from Llama-3.2-3B suggests this benefit extends across architectures. Second, Min-K\%++ exhibits directional reversal at small model scales, where the signal is actively misleading rather than merely ineffective. Third, domain-level $\gamma$ diagnosis is exploratory at current ensemble sizes: within-family analysis reveals directional heterogeneity across MMLU cognitive domains (Social Sciences reaching significance without multiple comparison correction), but reliable $\gamma$ recovery requires $J \geq 50$, well above current practice.
4	
5	\paragraph{Implications for practice.}
6	For benchmark developers, IRT-based calibration should be standard infrastructure: vanilla 2PL alone reduces MAE by 53\% without requiring contamination detection. For detection practitioners, Min-K\%++ should not be trusted below 1.5B parameters. For leaderboard operators, adding IRT to evaluation pipelines is a low-cost improvement that generalizes across architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross_family_validation}). For researchers, per-domain breakdowns should accompany aggregate scores, as contamination may affect cognitive domains differently.
7	
8	\paragraph{Future work.}
9	Scaling to $J \geq 50$ through broader model sampling would enable confirmatory domain-level diagnosis. Multidimensional IRT could model domain-specific ability dimensions that interact differently with contamination. Active signal selection via Fisher information optimization could reduce the requirement for full corpus access, enabling application to closed-source models.
10	
```

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	ContamCal operates in two stages: Stage~1 estimates per-item contamination exposure from multiple detection signals via training-free fusion; Stage~2 embeds these scores in a contamination-augmented IRT model to produce calibrated ability estimates with credible intervals.
5	
6	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
7	\label{sec:formulation}
8	
9	Let $y_{ijk} \in \{0,1\}$ denote the binary response of model~$j$ ($j=1,\ldots,J$) to item~$i$ ($i=1,\ldots,I_k$) on benchmark~$k$. Standard IRT assumes $P(y_{ijk}=1) = f(\theta_j, \boldsymbol{\xi}_i)$ where $\theta_j$ is latent ability and $\boldsymbol{\xi}_i$ collects item parameters. When training corpora contain benchmark items, the observed ability $\hat{\theta}_j$ confounds genuine capability with memorization.
10	
11	Our goal is twofold: (1)~estimate $\theta_j^{\text{clean}}$, the ability each model would exhibit had it never encountered test items during training, and (2)~diagnose contamination effects at domain granularity within each benchmark. We draw on item compromise IRT \citep{eckerly_2017_item_compromise,sinharay_2017_preknowledge} and explanatory IRT \citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}, adapting these frameworks through three structural modifications for LLM evaluation.
12	
13	\textit{Adaptation~1: Independent examinees $\to$ shared item pool.} Classical IRT assumes independent test-takers. In LLM evaluation, a fixed item set is shared across all models, and responses within a single model may exhibit residual correlations beyond the scalar~$\theta_j$ \citep{sanmartin_2009_irt}. We mitigate this by fitting per-benchmark models.
14	
15	\textit{Adaptation~2: Binary leakage $\to$ continuous exposure.} Item compromise IRT uses binary indicators \citep{sinharay_2017_preknowledge}. LLM contamination is graded: a model may have encountered an item zero, once, or hundreds of times. We replace binary indicators with continuous exposure scores $e_{ij} \in [0,1]$ derived from detection signals, following the explanatory IRT tradition \citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}.
16	
17	\textit{Adaptation~3: Stable ability $\to$ regime-conditional ability.} In educational testing, ability is approximately constant. For LLMs, the training regime (supervised fine-tuning, SFT, vs.\ reinforcement learning, RL) qualitatively changes both internal representations and signal reliability. We recover clean ability via counterfactual inference (setting exposure to zero) rather than regime-specific ability parameters.
18	
19	\subsection{Stage 1: Multi-Signal Exposure Mapping}
20	\label{sec:stage1}
21	
22	For each model~$j$ and item~$i$, we consider four candidate detection signals. \textbf{S1}~(Embedding similarity): cosine similarity between the item embedding and its nearest neighbor in the SFT training corpus. \textbf{S2}~(Min-K\%++): a membership inference statistic based on token-level surprisal \citep{zhang_2024_minkpp}. \textbf{S3}~(ConStat): the accuracy gap between original and paraphrased item variants \citep{dekoninck2024constat}. \textbf{S4}~(Self-Critique): negative entropy of model self-evaluation distributions, inspired by the finding that LLMs can assess their own knowledge \citep{kadavath2022}; concurrent work by \citet{tao2025detecting} uses a related but mechanistically distinct entropy collapse approach targeting RL-trained models. S3 requires paired paraphrased items as reference, limiting applicability to benchmarks with existing paraphrase sets. Our primary pipeline uses the remaining three signals ($\mathcal{S} = \{\text{S1, S2, S4}\}$), making it applicable to any benchmark without auxiliary data requirements. S2 and S4 exhibit known failure modes under RL post-training, which we characterize in \S\ref{sec:signal_failure}.
23	
24	We aggregate signals via Reciprocal Rank Fusion \citep[RRF;][]{cormack_2009_rrf}:
25	\begin{equation}
26	\label{eq:rrf}
27	    e_{ij} = \sum_{s \in \mathcal{S}} \frac{1}{\kappa + r_{s}(i)},
28	\end{equation}
29	where $r_s(i)$ is the rank of item~$i$ under signal~$s$ (rank~1 = most suspicious), $\kappa=60$ is the standard smoothing constant, and $|\mathcal{S}|=3$ in our primary configuration. Raw scores are min-max normalized per model to obtain $e_{ij} \in [0,1]$.
30	
```

We draw on item compromise IRT \\citep{eckerly_2017_item_compromise,sinharay_2017_preknowledge} and explanatory IRT \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}, adapting these frameworks through three structural modifications for LLM evaluation.\n\n\\textit{Adaptation~1: Independent examinees $\\to$ shared item pool.} Classical IRT assumes independent test-takers. In LLM evaluation, a fixed item set is shared across all models, and responses within a single model may exhibit residual correlations beyond the scalar~$\\theta_j$ \\citep{sanmartin_2009_irt}. We mitigate this by fitting per-benchmark models.\n\n\\textit{Adaptation~2: Binary leakage $\\to$ continuous exposure.} Item compromise IRT uses binary indicators \\citep{sinharay_2017_preknowledge}. LLM contamination is graded: a model may have encountered an item zero, once, or hundreds of times. We replace binary indicators with continuous exposure scores $e_{ij} \\in [0,1]$ derived from detection signals, following the explanatory IRT tradition \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}.\n\n\\textit{Adaptation~3: Stable ability $\\to$ regime-conditional ability.} In educational testing, ability is approximately constant. For LLMs, the training regime (supervised fine-tuning, SFT, vs.\\ reinforcement learning, RL) qualitatively changes both internal representations and signal reliability. We recover clean ability via counterfactual inference (setting exposure to zero) rather than regime-specific ability parameters.\n\n\\subsection{Stage 1: Multi-Signal Exposure Mapping}\n\\label{sec:stage1}\n\nFor each model~$j$ and item~$i$, we consider four candidate detection signals. \\textbf{S1}~(Embedding similarity): cosine similarity between the item embedding and its nearest neighbor in the SFT training corpus. \\textbf{S2}~(Min-K\\%++): a membership inference statistic based on token-level surprisal \\citep{zhang_2024_minkpp}. \\textbf{S3}~(ConStat): the accuracy gap between original and paraphrased item variants \\citep{dekoninck2024constat}. \\textbf{S4}~(Self-Critique): negative entropy of model self-evaluation distributions, inspired by the finding that LLMs can assess their own knowledge \\citep{kadavath2022}; concurrent work by \\citet{tao2025detecting} uses a related but mechanistically distinct entropy collapse approach targeting RL-trained models. S3 requires paired paraphrased items as reference, limiting applicability to benchmarks with existing paraphrase sets. Our primary pipeline uses the remaining three signals ($\\mathcal{S} = \\{\\text{S1, S2, S4}\\}$), making it applicable to any benchmark without auxiliary data requirements. S2 and S4 exhibit known failure modes under RL post-training, which we characterize in \\S\\ref{sec:signal_failure}.\n\nWe aggregate signals via Reciprocal Rank Fusion \\citep[RRF;][]{cormack_2009_rrf}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:rrf}\n    e_{ij} = \\sum_{s \\in \\mathcal{S}} \\frac{1}{\\kappa + r_{s}(i)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $r_s(i)$ is the rank of item~$i$ under signal~$s$ (rank~1 = most suspicious), $\\kappa=60$ is the standard smoothing constant, and $|\\mathcal{S}|=3$ in our primary configuration. Raw scores are min-max normalized per model to obtain $e_{ij} \\in [0,1]$.\n",

    }

```text
Good. The Method section correctly frames ContamCal as a tool for investigation, not as the primary contribution. The opening line says "ContamCal operates in two stages..." — it's descriptive of the tool, not claiming novelty.

The method section also properly cites prior work (item compromise IRT, explanatory IRT) and frames the adaptations as modifications of existing frameworks.

Now let me compile my full verification report. I have enough information.

Key issues I found:

1. **discussion.tex orphan** - exists but not in main.tex (conclusion.tex has "Discussion and Conclusion" combined section)


2. **ARC γ = -1.46 reported as significant despite PPP = 0.027** — this indicates model misfit but the significance claim remains
3. **CI coverage numbers in Limitations section are outdated** — currently states ~35% but the latest computation shows 45%/83%
4. **Cross-family comparison table still uses v4 data** with γ=+0.41 instead of the updated v5 value of γ=+0.35

I should also verify whether contamination-specific modeling is still being claimed as a contribution anywhere. Looking at Contribution 2 about "Multi-signal exposure detection via RRF" — this appears to still frame something as novel that might need closer examination.

The core tension here is that Contribution 2 positions RRF as a methodological contribution ("We develop a training-free signal fusion pipeline"), but then immediately undermines this by showing S1 dominates in practice. The honest caveat is there, but it creates an awkward framing: if S1 alone is sufficient, is the multi-signal pipeline really a contribution? The actual finding — that S1 dominates among fusion approaches — is the valuable insight, not the pipeline itself, yet the contribution is worded as method development rather than as a finding about signal hierarchy.

This was flagged as W8 and the Director confirmed it's been addressed. The current text directly states that S2 and S4 provide only marginal gains at larger scales and are actually detrimental at 0.5B, which is the honest acknowledgment needed. That resolves the concern.

Now I'm ready to compile the final verification report.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a very important result! Let me analyze:

## Full MMLU (14K items) Vanilla 2PL τ Results

**Key finding**: τ = 0.024 (p=0.893) — essentially zero correlation between IRT θ and dosage on full MMLU.

This is DRAMATICALLY different from the 300-item subsampled version (τ = 0.638, p=0.006).

**Why the massive difference?**

The worker's explanation is correct:
1. In the 300-item subsample, items were selected by RRF signal (contamination-relevant items). IRT on these items captures contamination-related ability variation.


2. In the full 14K items, only ~300 (2%) are contaminated. The other 98% of items dominate the ability estimate, measuring general MMLU knowledge unrelated to contamination. θ becomes "general MMLU ability" rather than "contamination-affected ability."

**Llama-3B catastrophic finding**: τ = -1.00 (perfect negative correlation with dosage). More contamination training → LOWER MMLU ability. This confirms my earlier observation: Qwen-format contamination training causes catastrophic forgetting in Llama for MMLU. Looking at the performance metrics across dosage levels, I'm seeing a clear pattern where increased contamination exposure systematically degrades overall MMLU performance.

**Qwen-3B** shows almost no sensitivity to contamination dosage—ability estimates stay relatively flat across the range, suggesting the SFT data volume is too small to meaningfully shift their general knowledge measurements.

**Qwen-1.5B** exhibits a slight upward trend but it's not statistically significant, so contamination effects are minimal here too.

The key takeaway for the paper is that contamination training has dramatically different effects depending on the model: catastrophic for Llama but essentially neutral for Qwen variants.

Now I'm realizing there's a nuance in how the paper frames vanilla 2PL's effectiveness. The existing Table 2 reports τ = 0.638 on the 300-item subset, which is the correct signal for ContamCal since it's inherently item-selective. But when I look at the full MMLU set, τ drops to 0.024—vanilla 2PL without item-level exposure information basically can't detect contamination at scale. This actually validates ContamCal's core contribution: you need the RRF signal to identify which items matter before IRT calibration becomes useful. The paper currently suggests vanilla 2PL provides calibration on its own, but that's not quite right—it works for reducing measurement error (MAE), just not for contamination detection without the item filtering step. Looking at Table 2 more carefully, both vanilla 2PL and ContamCal give identical τ values of 0.638 on the 300-item subset because the ranking metric doesn't benefit from the contamination parameter in this pre-selected setting. The full MMLU results confirm that without item selection, vanilla 2PL fails to rank models by dosage — so RRF selection is what actually enables the ability estimates to capture contamination-related variation. The Rhat diagnostics look solid with all values well below 1.01 despite using only 2 chains from the completed runs.

This is a legitimate finding that strengthens the paper's narrative by clarifying that IRT calibration works, but ranking by dosage specifically requires the item-level selection step. I should make sure the paper addresses the gap between the 300-item τ of 0.638 and the 14K-item τ of 0.024 — either in the Discussion or Appendix — so reviewers have a clear answer if they ask about full MMLU performance.
```

### Assistant

_source line 1838 · 2026-05-17T15:54:16.225Z_

**重大发现**。Full MMLU 14K items vanilla 2PL τ = 0.024 (p=0.89) — 与 300-item subsampled τ = 0.638 (p=0.006) 完全不同。

**审查评估**：

1. **结果可信**：2 chains Rhat < 1.003，收敛良好。θ 值合理（Qwen 3B > 1.5B > Llama 3B）。
2. **Worker 解释正确**：14K items 中仅 ~2% contaminated，clean items 主导 θ estimate，信号被稀释。
3. **Llama catastrophic forgetting确认**：τ = -1.00（完美负相关），raw acc 从 d000=0.455 到 d100=0.341，contamination SFT 摧毁 MMLU 能力。
4. **Qwen-3B θ 基本恒定**：dosage 对 full-MMLU ability 几乎无影响（Δθ < 0.12）。

**对论文的关键影响**：

Table 2 的 MMLU τ = 0.638 来自 300-item **RRF-selected** subsample。论文需要透明说明这一点。如果审稿人问 "full MMLU τ?"，答案是"近零"——这恰恰证明了 **item-level exposure selection（RRF Stage 1）的必要性**：没有 signal-guided item selection，vanilla IRT 无法分离 contamination 信号。

这实际上**加强**了 ContamCal 的 value proposition：
- 论文当前 framing: "vanilla 2PL alone provides calibration" 
- 新 nuance: 这只在 signal-selected items 上成立（300-item）；full benchmark 上 IRT 仍需 exposure signals 作为 item filter

建议在 Discussion 或 Appendix 加一段 transparency disclosure。

{
  "text": "[severity: warning] Full MMLU τ 结果：vanilla 2PL on 14K items τ=0.024 vs 300-item τ=0.638\n\n## 发现\n\nWorker 从 crashed MCMC 的 2 条已完成 chains 提取了 full MMLU (14,042 items, 20 models) vanilla 2PL 结果：\n\n- Full MMLU τ = 0.024 (p=0.893) — 近零\n- 300-item subsampled τ = 0.638 (p=0.006) — 高度显著\n\n差异原因：14K items 中仅 ~2% 为 contaminated items，clean items 主导 ability estimate。\n\n## 对论文 framing 的影响\n\nTable 2 的 MMLU τ=0.638 来自 RRF-selected 300 items。论文当前 framing \"vanilla 2PL alone provides calibration\" 在 full benchmark 上的 τ 维度不成立。\n\n**但这不是 contradiction**——而是 clarification：\n- MAE（校准精度）：full MMLU vanilla 2PL 仍可提供 MAE 改善（通过 scale separation）\n- τ（dosage ranking）：需要 RRF item selection 才有效\n\n## 建议\n\n1. 论文中（§5.1 或 Discussion）应明确：Table 2 的 τ 数据来自 \"RRF-selected items (I=300)\"\n2. 可在 Appendix 加 transparency 段落：full-benchmark IRT without signal selection 无法 rank by dosage（τ ≈ 0），证明 Stage 1 signal fusion 的必要性\n3. 这实际上**加强** ContamCal pipeline 的整体 value——不是\"vanilla 2PL 够了\"，而是\"RRF item selection + vanilla 2PL 的组合够了，γ 是 optional diagnostic\"\n\n## 附加发现\n\nLlama-3B τ = -1.00 (perfect negative correlation)：contamination SFT 在 full MMLU 上导致 catastrophic forgetting（raw acc 0.455→0.341）。这为 cross-family analysis 中 Llama θ 递减提供了 full-MMLU 验证。\n\n## Rhat 说明\n\n仅用 2 chains（crashed run 中完成的 chains 3/4），Rhat 0.9995-1.003，收敛可接受但非理想。如需正式引用此数字，建议服务器恢复后重跑 4 chains。",
  "project": "contamcal-irt-calibration"
}

**14k τ 发现的 framing 影响正式评估请求**

Full MMLU 14k τ=0.024 vs 300-item τ=0.637 的发现需要比一条 warning 更深入的评估。请回答以下问题：

1. **Stage 1 item selection 是否可以升级为更强的 contribution？**
   - 14k 结果直接证明：没有 RRF item selection，vanilla 2PL 在全量 benchmark 上完全无效
   - 这是否意味着 Contribution 2 (RRF) 应该从 "marginal, S1 dominates" 升为 "necessary preprocessing"？
   - Paper Agent 已将 C2 改为 "Exposure-based item selection via RRF"——这个定位足够强吗？

2. **论文 story 是否因此更强了？**
   - 原来："vanilla 2PL alone suffices"（但这只在 filtered items 上成立）
   - 现在："RRF item selection + vanilla 2PL suffices, γ is optional diagnostic"
   - 这是一个更精确、更诚实的 story。是否比原版更有说服力？

3. **这个发现是否暴露了弱点？**
   - 审稿人可能追问："那 RRF 的 300-item selection 阈值怎么定的？不同阈值结果如何？"——W10 sensitivity 正在跑
   - 2-chain MCMC 结果的可靠性——是否需要在论文中标注为 preliminary？

4. **有无进一步利用这个发现的机会？**
   - 例如：展示不同 item selection 比例（100/300/500/1000/all）的 τ 变化曲线——如果有数据的话

请给出综合评估和建议。
